\exp\{-\frac{u}{2(1+Cx^{m})}\}=\exp\{-ug(x)\}\geq\exp\{-ug(x_{0}+1-\delta)\}=:e^{-c_{1}u},

where
c_{1}:=g(x_{0}+1-\delta).

Then

\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(x)dx\geq\int\limits_{I_{\delta}}\pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(x)dx\geq\int\limits_{I_{\delta}}A_{\delta}e^{-c_{1}u}dx=A_{\delta}\cdot\delta e^{-c_{1}u}=:K_{1}e^{-c_{1}u}.

Then

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}((0,x_{0}))=\frac{\int\limits_{0}^{x_{0}}\pi_{u}(x)dx}{\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\pi_{u}(x)dx}\leq\frac{K_{0}e^{-c_{0}u}}{K_{1}e^{-c_{1}u}}=\frac{K_{0}}{K_{1}}e^{-(c_{0}-c_{1})u}.

That is for large
u
and
x_{0}

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}((0,x_{0}))\leq e^{-cu}
for some
c:=c_{0}-c_{1}>0
. For

x=\lambda x_{u}\geq x_{0}
. Then
dx=x_{u}d\lambda
. Recall

\phi_{u}(\lambda)=m\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}}.

Then

\displaystyle-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=-x_{u}(m\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}})

\displaystyle=-(m(\lambda x_{u})+\frac{x_{u}A_{u}}{\lambda^{m}A_{u}+1})

\displaystyle=-(m(\lambda x_{u})+\frac{u}{2(1+\lambda^{m}A_{u})})

\displaystyle=-(m(\lambda x_{u})+\frac{u}{2(1+C(\lambda x_{u})^{m})})

\displaystyle=-f_{u}(\lambda x_{u}).

Therefore
\exp\{-f_{u}(\lambda x_{u})\}=\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}.
Then

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda):=x_{u}\pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda x_{u})

\displaystyle\leq K_{2}x_{u}(\lambda x_{u})^{\gamma}(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}

\displaystyle=K_{2}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}\lambda^{\gamma}(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}

\displaystyle=K_{2}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}q_{u}(\lambda),q_{u}(\lambda):=\lambda^{\gamma}(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}.

Similarly,
\tilde{\pi}_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda)\geq K_{1}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}q_{u}(\lambda)
. That is

K_{1}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}q_{u}(\lambda)\leq\tilde{\pi}_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda)\leq K_{2}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}q_{u}(\lambda).

Consider measurable
S\subseteq[x_{0}x_{u}^{-1},\infty).
Then

\displaystyle\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(S|X\geq x_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\lambda\in S|X\geq x_{0},U=u)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int\limits_{S}\tilde{\pi}_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda)d\lambda}{\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}\tilde{\pi}_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda)d\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{2}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}\int\limits_{S}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}{K_{1}x_{u}^{\gamma+1}\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{2}}{K_{1}}\frac{\int\limits_{S}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}{\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}.

Similarly,

\Pi_{u|\boldsymbol{k}}(S|X\geq x_{0})\geq\frac{K_{1}}{K_{2}}\frac{\int\limits_{S}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}{\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}.

That is

\frac{K_{1}}{K_{2}}\frac{\int\limits_{S}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}{\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}\leq\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(S|X\geq x_{0})\leq\frac{K_{2}}{K_{1}}\frac{\int\limits_{S}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}{\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda}.

Now set
S=S_{\xi}:=\{|\lambda-1|\geq\xi\}\cap[x_{u}^{-1}x_{0},\infty)
. Recall
\phi_{u}(\lambda)=m\lambda+(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-1}.
Then

\displaystyle\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=m-(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}(m\lambda^{m-1})

\displaystyle=m-\frac{m\lambda^{m-1}}{(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=m-(m\lambda^{m-1})(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}.

This also implies

\displaystyle\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=[-m\lambda^{m-1}][-2(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-3}](m\lambda^{m-1})+(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}[-m(m-1)\lambda^{m-2}]

\displaystyle=2m\lambda^{m-1}(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-3}m\lambda^{m-1}-(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}m(m-1)\lambda^{m-2}

\displaystyle=2m^{2}\lambda^{2m-2}(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-3}-m(m-1)\lambda^{m-2}(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{m-2}[2m^{2}\lambda^{m}(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-3}-m(m-1)(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}]

\displaystyle=m\lambda^{m-2}[2m\lambda^{m}(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-3}-(m-1)(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{-2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{m\lambda^{m-2}}{(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{3}}(2m\lambda^{m}-(m-1)(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\frac{m\lambda^{m-2}}{(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{3}}(2m\lambda^{m}-m\lambda^{m}-mA_{u}^{-1}+\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{m\lambda^{m-2}}{(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{3}}((m+1)\lambda^{m}-(m-1)A_{u}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{m\lambda^{m-2}}{(\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1})^{3}}((m+1)\lambda^{m}-(m-1)A_{u}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{m\lambda^{m-2}}{(\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{3}}((m+1)\lambda^{m}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}).

For
\lambda\geq 2^{-1}
we have
\lambda^{m}\geq 2^{-m}
. Since
A_{u}\rightarrow\infty
, for all large
u

(m+1)\lambda^{m}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}\geq\frac{m+1}{2^{m}}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}>\frac{m+1}{2^{m+1}}>0.

Therefore
\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\lambda)>0
on
[2^{-1},\infty)
. So
\phi_{u}
is strictly convex and has at most one critical point on
[2^{-1},\infty).
At
\lambda=1,

\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}(1)=m-\frac{m}{(1+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{2}}>0\text{ since }(1+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{2}>1.

For
\lambda=2^{-1}
and sufficiently large
u
,
A_{u}\geq 2^{(m+1)}
iff
A_{u}^{-1}\leq 2^{-(m+1)}
. Therefore,

(\frac{1}{2})^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}\leq(\frac{1}{2})^{m}+(\frac{1}{2})^{m+1}=(\frac{1}{2})^{m}+\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2})^{m}=\frac{3}{2}(\frac{1}{2})^{m}.

Thus,

\frac{(\frac{1}{2})^{m-1}}{((\frac{1}{2})^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{2}}\geq\frac{(\frac{1}{2})^{m-1}}{[\frac{3}{2}(\frac{1}{2})^{m}]^{2}}=(\frac{1}{2})^{m-1}\frac{4}{9}(\frac{1}{2})^{-2m}=\frac{4}{9}(\frac{1}{2})^{-m-1}=\frac{4}{9}2^{m+1}.

Hence for large
u
,

\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}(\frac{1}{2})=m(1-\frac{(\frac{1}{2})^{m-1}}{((\frac{1}{2})^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{2}})\leq m(1-\frac{4}{9}2^{m+1})<0,\text{ since }

\displaystyle 1-\frac{4}{9}2^{m+1}<0

\displaystyle\iff 1<\frac{4}{9}2^{m+1}

\displaystyle\iff\frac{9}{4}<2^{m+1}

\displaystyle\iff\log(\frac{9}{4})<(m+1)\log(2)

\displaystyle\iff\frac{\log(\frac{9}{4})}{\log(2)}-1<m,

which is true since
m\geq 1
. So for sufficiently large
u

\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}(2^{-1})<0
and
\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}(1)>0
. By continuity
\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}
has a zero in
(2^{-1},1)
. By strict convexity on
[2^{-1},\infty)
that zero is unique and the unique minimizer
\lambda_{u}^{*}\in(2^{-1},1).
From above we know as
A_{u}\rightarrow\infty,

\phi_{u}(\lambda)\rightarrow\phi_{\infty}(\lambda)
pointwise on
(0,\infty).
Furthermore the convergence is uniform on
[2^{-1},2]
because

|\frac{1}{\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}}-\lambda^{-m}|=\frac{\frac{1}{A_{u}}}{\lambda^{m}(\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}})}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{A_{u}}}{(\frac{1}{2})^{2m}}=\frac{2^{2m}}{A_{u}}\rightarrow 0.

We know
\phi_{\infty}(\lambda)
has a unique minimizer at
\lambda=1
. Therefore we know that

|\lambda_{u}^{*}-1|<\epsilon,\epsilon>0,
with
\lambda_{u}^{*}\rightarrow 1.
Therefore for fixed
\epsilon\in(0,2^{-1})
for all sufficiently large u,

|\lambda-1|=|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}+\lambda_{u}^{*}-1|\leq|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|+|\lambda_{u}^{*}-1|.

Then
|\lambda-1|\geq\epsilon,|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq\epsilon-|\lambda_{u}^{*}-1|.
Then for sufficiently large
u
we have

|\lambda_{u}^{*}-1|\leq 2^{-1}\epsilon.
This gives
|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq\epsilon-2^{-1}\epsilon=2^{-1}\epsilon.
That is for sufficiently large
u
,
|\lambda-1|\geq\epsilon
which implies
|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq 2^{-1}\epsilon.
Therefore we can focus on bounding the posterior mass outside
[\lambda_{u}^{*}-2^{-1}\epsilon,\lambda{u}^{*}+2^{-1}\epsilon].
Let
\eta_{u}:=x_{u}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
B_{u}:=[\lambda_{u}^{*}-\eta_{u},\lambda_{u}^{*}+\eta_{u}].
For all sufficiently large
u
,
\lambda_{u}^{*}\in(2^{-1},1)
and
\eta_{u}\rightarrow 0
. Hence
B_{u}\subset[2^{-1},2]
and

B_{u}\subset[x_{u}^{-1}x_{0},\infty).
We will show that
\exists c_{in}>0
such that for all large
u

\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\geq\int\limits_{B_{u}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\geq c_{in}\eta_{u}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}.

Since
B_{u}\subset[2^{-1},2],\lambda^{\gamma}\geq\min\limits_{\lambda\in[2^{-1},2]}\lambda^{\gamma}=2^{-|\gamma|}=:c_{\gamma}>0,\forall\lambda\in B_{u}
. For
\lambda\in[2^{-1},2],

1+A_{u}\lambda^{m}\leq 1+A_{u}2^{m}\leq 2^{m}+A_{u}2^{m}=2^{m}(1+A_{u})
for
A_{u}\geq 1
which is true for sufficiently large
u
. Hence
(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\geq 2^{-\frac{mp}{2}}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}=:c_{p}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}
. On
[2^{-1},2]
for
A_{u}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\lambda^{m-2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{|m-2|},

\displaystyle(\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{-3}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-3m}\leq 2^{3m},

and

\displaystyle(m+1)\lambda^{m}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}

\displaystyle\leq(m+1)\lambda^{m}\leq(m+1)2^{m}.

Therefore, for all large
u
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\lambda\in[2^{-1},2]}\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\lambda)\leq M

\displaystyle:=m2^{|m-2|}2^{3m}(m+1)2^{m}

\displaystyle=m(m+1)2^{|m-2|+3m+m}

\displaystyle=m(m+1)2^{|m-2|+4m}<\infty,\text{ which is independent of }u.

By Taylor’s theorem,

\displaystyle\phi_{u}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime}}(\lambda_{u}^{*})(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\xi)(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\sup\limits_{\xi\in[\frac{1}{2},2]}\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\xi)(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{M}{2}(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})^{2}.

For
\lambda\in B_{u},\lambda_{u}^{*}-\eta_{u}\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_{u}^{*}+\eta_{u}
iff
|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\leq\eta_{u}
. Then,

\phi_{u}(\lambda)\leq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{M}{2}\eta_{u}^{2}=\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{M}{2x_{u}}.

Thus

\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}\geq\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}\exp\{-\frac{M}{2}\},\forall\lambda\in B_{u}.

So

q_{u}(\lambda)\geq c_{\gamma}c_{p}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}e^{-\frac{M}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\},\text{ for }\lambda\in B_{u}.

Then

\int\limits_{B_{u}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\geq 2\eta_{u}c_{\gamma}c_{p}e^{-\frac{M}{2}}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\},|B_{u}|=2\eta_{u}.

That is

\int\limits_{B_{u}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\geq c_{in}\eta_{u}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\},c_{in}:=2c_{\gamma}c_{p}e^{-\frac{M}{2}}>0.

Fix
\epsilon\in(0,2^{-1})
. Furthermore,
O_{u}:=\{\lambda\geq x_{u}^{-1}x_{0}:|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq 2^{-1}\epsilon\}.
Choose
b>2
and
a\in(0,2^{-1})
such that
(2a^{m})^{-1}>m+2
. Then
O_{u}=O_{u}^{(L)}\cup O_{u}^{(M)}\cup O_{u}^{(R)}
where
O_{u}^{(L)}:=O_{u}\cap(0,a],O_{u}^{(M)}:=O_{u}\cap[a,b],
and
O_{u}^{(R)}:=O_{u}\cap[b,\infty).
On
[a,b]
for large
u
, since
\lambda\in[a,b],

\lambda^{m-2}\geq\ell_{1}=\begin{cases}a^{m-2},&m\geq 2,\\
b^{-1},&m=1\end{cases}.

For sufficiently large
u,A_{u}\geq 1
. Hence
A_{u}^{-1}\leq 1
. Then for
\lambda\in[a,b],

\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}\leq b^{m}+1\implies(\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}})^{-3}\geq(b^{m}+1)^{-3}.

For
\lambda\in[a,b],\lambda^{m}\geq a^{m}.
So

(m+1)\lambda^{m}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}\geq(m+1)a^{m}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}.

If
m=1,A_{u}^{-1}(m-1)=0
. If
m\geq 2,
then for sufficiently large
u
,

A_{u}\geq\frac{2(m-1)}{(m+1)a^{m}}.

Then

\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}\leq\frac{m-1}{\frac{2(m-1)}{(m+1)a^{m}}}=\frac{(m+1)a^{m}}{2}.

This implies

(m+1)\lambda^{m}-\frac{m-1}{A_{u}}\geq(m+1)a^{m}-\frac{(m+1)a^{m}}{2}=\frac{(m+1)a^{m}}{2}.

Therefore

\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\lambda)\geq m\ell_{1}(b^{m}+1)^{-3}\frac{(m+1)a^{m}}{2}=:c_{0}.

So,
\inf\limits_{\lambda\in[a,b]}\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\lambda)\geq c_{0}>0
. By Taylor’s theorem

\phi_{u}(\lambda)\geq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\phi_{u}^{\prime}(\lambda_{u}^{*})(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{\phi_{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}(\xi)}{2}(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})^{2}\geq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{c_{0}}{2}(\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*})^{2}.

Hence on
O_{u}^{(M)},|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq 2^{-1}\epsilon.
Then,

\phi_{u}(\lambda)\geq\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+\frac{c_{0}}{2}(\frac{\epsilon}{2})^{2}=\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+c_{m}(\epsilon),c_{m}(\epsilon):=8^{-1}c_{0}\epsilon^{2}.

Note that on
[a,b]

\lambda^{\gamma}\leq c_{a,b}:=\begin{cases}b^{\gamma},&\gamma>0,\\
1&\gamma=0,\\
a^{\gamma},&\gamma<0\end{cases}.

Furthermore
(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\leq(1+A_{u}a^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}.
Observe

1+A_{u}a^{m}\geq a^{m}+A_{u}a^{m}=a^{m}(1+A_{u}).
This implies

(1+A_{u}a^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\leq a^{-\frac{mp}{2}}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}=(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}c^{\prime\prime}(a),a^{-\frac{mp}{2}}:=c^{\prime\prime}(a).

Therefore

\displaystyle\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(M)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(M)}}\lambda^{\gamma}(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c_{a,b}c^{\prime\prime}(a)(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(M)}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}d\lambda.

Note that

\displaystyle\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+c_{m}(\epsilon))\}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}\exp\{-x_{u}c_{m}(\epsilon)\}.

Then

\displaystyle\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(M)}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda)\}

\displaystyle\leq|O_{u}^{(M)}|\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}\exp\{-x_{u}c_{m}(\epsilon)\}

\displaystyle\leq(b-a)\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}\exp\{-x_{u}c_{m}(\epsilon)\}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(M)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq c_{a,b}c^{\prime\prime}(a)(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}(b-a)\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+c_{m}(\epsilon))\}

\displaystyle=K_{m}(\epsilon)(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+c_{m}(\epsilon))\},

K_{m}(\epsilon):=c_{a,b}c^{\prime\prime}(a)(b-a).
We will now show that

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(R)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq K_{R}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R})\}.

Consider
\lambda\geq b
. We know that
\phi_{u}(\lambda)\geq m\lambda
and for large
u
,

(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\leq(A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}=A_{u}^{-\frac{p}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{mp}{2}}.

For large
u,A_{u}\geq 1
. Then,

1+A_{u}\leq 2A_{u}\implies(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\geq 2^{-\frac{p}{2}}A_{u}^{-\frac{p}{2}}\implies A_{u}^{-\frac{p}{2}}\leq 2^{\frac{p}{2}}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

Therefore,

(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\leq 2^{\frac{p}{2}}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{mp}{2}}=c_{p}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{mp}{2}},c_{p}:=2^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Thus for
\lambda\geq b
,

\displaystyle q_{u}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\gamma}c_{p}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\lambda^{-\frac{mp}{2}}\exp\{-m\lambda x_{u}\}

\displaystyle=c_{p}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\lambda^{\gamma-\frac{mp}{2}}\exp\{-m\lambda x_{u}\}.

Let
r:=\gamma-2^{-1}(mp)
,
a:=mx_{u}>0
and
I:=\int\limits_{b}^{\infty}\lambda^{r}e^{-a\lambda}d\lambda.
Let
s:=a(\lambda-b)=a\lambda-ab
which implies
\lambda=b+a^{-1}s
. Then
d\lambda=a^{-1}ds
. Then

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(b+\frac{s}{a})^{r}\exp\{-a(b+\frac{s}{a})\}\frac{ds}{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(b+\frac{s}{a})^{r}\exp\{-ab-s\}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-ab}}{a}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(b+\frac{s}{a})^{r}e^{-s}ds.

Firstly consider
r\leq 0.
Since
b+a^{-1}s\geq b
and
x\rightarrow x^{r}
is decreasing,
(b+a^{-1}s)^{r}\leq b^{r}.
So,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-ab}}{a}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}b^{r}e^{-s}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-ab}b^{r}}{a}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-ab}b^{r}}{a}[-e^{-s}]

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{r}e^{-ab}}{a}.

Now consider
r\geq 0
. Recall
(x+y)^{r}\leq 2^{r}(x^{r}+y^{r})
for
x,y\geq 0.
So

(b+\frac{s}{a})^{r}\leq 2^{r}(b^{r}+(\frac{s}{a})^{r}).

Then

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-ab}}{a}2^{r}(b^{r}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}ds+\frac{1}{a^{r}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}s^{r}e^{-s}ds)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-ab}2^{r}}{a}(b^{r}+\frac{1}{a^{r}}\Gamma(r+1)).

For sufficiently large
u,a\geq 1,
so
a^{-r}\Gamma(r+1)\leq\Gamma(r+1)
. Hence

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-ab}}{a}2^{r}(b^{r}+\Gamma(r+1))

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{r,b}}{mx_{u}}e^{-mbx_{u}},C_{r,b}:=2^{r}(b^{r}+\Gamma(r+1)).

So for
r\leq 0

I\leq\frac{b^{r}}{mx_{u}}e^{-mbx_{u}}\text{ and }r\geq 0\text{ implies }I\leq\frac{C_{r,b}}{mx_{u}}e^{-mbx_{u}}.

As
x_{u}\rightarrow\infty
as
u\rightarrow\infty
, we have for sufficiently large
u_{0},x_{u}^{-1}\leq x_{u_{0}}^{-1}
. So

I\leq\frac{C_{r,b}}{mx_{u_{0}}}e^{-mbx_{u}}=:Ke^{-mbx_{u}}.

So

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{r,b}}{a}e^{-ab},\tilde{C}_{r,b}=\begin{cases}b^{r},&r\leq 0,\\
2^{r}(b^{r}+\Gamma(r+1)),&r\geq 0\end{cases}

\displaystyle=K_{r,b}e^{-ab},\text{ with }K_{r,b}:=\frac{C_{r,b}}{mx_{u_{0}}}

\displaystyle=K_{r,b}e^{-mbx_{u}}.

Hence

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(R)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq C(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}K_{r,b}e^{-mbx_{u}}=K(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}e^{-mbx_{u}},K:=CK_{r,b}.

We know that
\lambda_{u}^{*}\rightarrow 1
. Then for sufficiently large
u
,
\lambda_{u}^{*}\in[0.5,1.5]
. On that interval

\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})=m\lambda_{u}^{*}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{u}^{*^{m}}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}}\leq\frac{3m}{2}+\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{2})^{m}}=\frac{3m}{2}+2^{m}.

Therefore
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\leq M
for sufficiently large
u
. Since
b>2,mb>m+1
. So

C_{R}:=2^{-1}(mb-(m+1))>0
. Then
2C_{R}+(m+1)=mb.
Since
\lambda_{u}^{*}\rightarrow 1
which implies
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\rightarrow\phi_{\infty}(1)=m+1
. Then
\exists u_{1}
such that
\forall u\geq u_{1},\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\leq(m+1)+C_{R}
which implies
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R}\leq(m+1)+2C_{R}
which implies
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R}\leq mb
. Then

\displaystyle x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R})\leq mbx_{u}

\displaystyle\implies-

\displaystyle x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R})\geq-mbx_{u}

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R})\}\geq\exp\{-mbx_{u}\}.

Therefore

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(R)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq K_{R}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{R})\}.

Now we will show that

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(L)}}q_{u}(\lambda)\leq K_{L}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}.

Note that

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(L)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq\int\limits_{0}^{a}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda.

Let
\lambda_{*}:=A_{u}^{-\frac{1}{m}}
which implies
\lambda_{*}^{m}=A_{u}^{-1}
. Observe that for sufficiently large
u,\lambda_{*}<a.
Then

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(L)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda_{*}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda+\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda.

If
\lambda\leq\lambda_{*}
then
\lambda^{m}\leq A_{u}^{-1}.
So
\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1}\leq 2A_{u}^{-1}.
Hence

\phi_{u}(\lambda)\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{m}+\frac{1}{A_{u}}}\geq\frac{1}{\frac{2}{A_{u}}}=\frac{A_{u}}{2}.

Furthermore
(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\leq 1.
Therefore

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda_{*}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda_{*}}\lambda^{\gamma}\exp\{-x_{u}\frac{A_{u}}{2}\}d\lambda

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}A_{u}}{2}\}\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda_{*}}\lambda^{\gamma}d\lambda

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}A_{u}}{2}\}[\frac{\lambda^{\gamma+1}}{\gamma+1}]_{0}^{\lambda_{*}}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}A_{u}}{2}\}\frac{\lambda_{*}^{\gamma+1}}{\gamma+1}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{u}{4}\}\frac{\lambda_{*}^{\gamma+1}}{\gamma+1}.

Since
\lambda_{*}=A_{u}^{-\frac{1}{m}}\rightarrow 0
and
\gamma+1>0,
we have
\lambda_{*}^{\gamma+1}\leq 2^{-1}
for sufficiently large
u
. So for large
u
,
\lambda^{\gamma+1}(\gamma+1)^{-1}\leq(\gamma+1)^{-1}=:c
. Therefore

\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda_{*}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq ce^{-\frac{u}{4}}.

If
\lambda\geq\lambda_{*}
then
\lambda^{m}\geq A_{u}^{-1}
. Hence
2\lambda^{m}\geq\lambda^{m}+A_{u}^{-1}.
Therefore
\phi_{u}(\lambda)\geq(2\lambda^{m})^{-1}.
Thus

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}\lambda^{\gamma}(1+A_{u}\lambda^{m})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2\lambda^{m}}\}d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}\lambda^{\gamma}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2\lambda^{m}}\}d\lambda.

Let

t:=\frac{x_{u}}{2\lambda^{m}}\implies\lambda=(\frac{x_{u}}{2t})^{\frac{1}{m}}=(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{1}{m}}t^{-\frac{1}{m}}.

Then

d\lambda=(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{1}{m}}(-\frac{1}{m}t^{-\frac{1}{m}-1})dt\text{ and }\lambda^{\gamma}=(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{\gamma}{m}}t^{-\frac{\gamma}{m}}.

So

\lambda^{\gamma}d\lambda=(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{\gamma}{m}}t^{-\frac{\gamma}{m}}(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{1}{m}}(-\frac{1}{m})t^{-\frac{1}{m}-1}dt=-\frac{1}{m}(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{(\gamma+1)}{m}}t^{-\frac{\gamma+1}{m}-1}dt.

When
\lambda=a,t_{a}:=(2a^{m})^{-1}x_{u}
and when
\lambda=\lambda_{*},t_{*}=(2\lambda_{*}^{m})^{-1}x_{u}
. Then

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}\lambda^{\gamma}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2\lambda^{m}}\}d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{t_{*}}^{t_{a}}-\frac{1}{m}(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}t^{-\frac{\gamma+1}{m}-1}e^{-t}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}\int\limits_{t_{a}}^{t_{*}}t^{-\frac{\gamma+1}{m}-1}e^{-t}dt.

Since
\gamma>-1,m^{-1}(\gamma+1)>0
. So
-m^{-1}(\gamma+1)<0
. Hence
\forall t\geq 1,t^{-\frac{\gamma+1}{m}-1}\leq 1.
We know that
t_{a}=(2a^{m})^{-1}x_{u}\rightarrow\infty
as
u\rightarrow\infty.
So for all sufficiently large u
t_{a}\geq 1.
Therefore

\int\limits_{t_{a}}^{t_{*}}t^{-\frac{\gamma+1}{m}-1}e^{-t}dt\leq\int\limits_{t_{a}}^{t_{*}}e^{-t}dt\leq\int\limits_{t_{a}}^{\infty}e^{-t}dt=[-e^{-t}]_{t_{a}}^{\infty}=e^{-t_{a}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}\lambda^{\gamma}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2\lambda^{m}}\}d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}(\frac{x_{u}}{2})^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}e^{-t_{a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m2^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}}x_{u}^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2a^{m}}\}

\displaystyle=Kx_{u}^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2a^{m}}\},K:=\frac{1}{m}2^{-\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}.

Let

4C_{L}:=\frac{1}{2a^{m}}-(m+2).

Then,

4C_{L}>0\iff\frac{1}{2a^{m}}-(m+2)>0\iff\frac{1}{2a^{m}}>m+2,\text{by definition of }a.

Let

M_{u}:=\min\limits_{\lambda\in[\frac{1}{2},2]}\phi_{u}(\lambda)=\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\text{ and }M_{\infty}:=\min\limits_{\lambda\in[\frac{1}{2},2]}\phi_{\infty}(\lambda)=\phi_{\infty}(1)=m+1.

Recall for functions
f
and
g
on a compact set
K
that
|\min\limits_{k}f-\min\limits_{k}g|\leq\sup\limits_{k}|f-g|.
Then
|\min\limits_{\lambda\in[\frac{1}{2},2]}\phi_{u}-\min\limits_{\lambda\in[\frac{1}{2},2]}\phi_{\infty}|=|\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})-(m+1)|\leq\sup\limits_{\lambda\in[\frac{1}{2},2]}|\phi_{u}(\lambda)-\phi_{\infty}(\lambda)|\rightarrow 0.
Therefore
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\rightarrow m+1.
Then for all large
u,\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\leq m+2
. Hence

\displaystyle\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2a^{m}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-x_{u}((m+2)+4C_{L})\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+4C_{L})\}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}\lambda^{\gamma}\exp\{-\frac{x_{u}}{2\lambda^{m}}\}d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq Kx_{u}^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+4C_{L})\}

\displaystyle=K\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}[x_{u}^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}e^{-4C_{L}x_{u}}].

Now, for all sufficiently large
u
we will show that
x_{u}^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}\exp\{-4C_{L}x_{u}\}\leq\exp\{-C_{L}x_{u}\}
. That is

\displaystyle x_{u}^{\frac{\gamma+1}{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{3C_{L}x_{u}\}

\displaystyle\iff\frac{\gamma+1}{m}\log(x_{u})

\displaystyle\leq 3C_{L}x_{u}

\displaystyle\iff\frac{\log(x_{u})}{x_{u}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3mC_{L}}{\gamma+1}\text{ which is true for sufficiently large }u.

So ,

\int\limits_{\lambda_{*}}^{a}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq K\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}.

Then

\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(L)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\leq ce^{-\frac{u}{4}}+K\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}.

We know
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\leq\phi_{u}(1),
but
\phi_{u}(1)\leq m+1.
Hence
\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L}\leq m+1+C_{L}
. Let
B:=m+1+C_{L}>0
. We want

\displaystyle\exp\{-\frac{u}{4}\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}\text{ i.e. }

\displaystyle\frac{u}{4}

\displaystyle\geq x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L}).

Then it is sufficient to check that
4^{-1}u\geq Bx_{u}.
That is

\frac{C}{2}x_{u}^{m+1}\geq Bx_{u}\iff\frac{C}{2}x_{u}^{m}\geq B\text{ which holds for sufficiently large }u.

Then,

\displaystyle\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(L)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq(c+K)\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}

\displaystyle=K_{L}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}.

This gives

\displaystyle\int\limits_{O_{u}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(L)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda+\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(M)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda+\int\limits_{O_{u}^{(R)}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}[K_{M}\exp\{-C_{M}x_{u}\}

\displaystyle+K_{R}\exp\{-C_{R}x_{u}\}]+K_{L}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{L})\}.

Let
C_{\text{min}}:=\min\{C_{M},C_{R},C_{L}\}
. Then
e^{-C_{M}x_{u}}\leq e^{-C_{\text{min}}x_{u}},e^{-C_{R}x_{u}}\leq e^{-C_{\text{min}}x_{u}}
,

and
e^{-C_{L}x_{u}}\leq e^{-C_{\text{min}}x_{u}}
. So

\displaystyle\int\limits_{O_{u}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}e^{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{\text{min}})}(K_{M}+K_{R})+K_{L}e^{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{\text{min}})}

\displaystyle=e^{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{\text{min}})}[(K_{M}+K_{R})(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+K_{L}]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{\text{min}})}[K_{M}+K_{R}+K_{L}]

\displaystyle=K_{\epsilon}e^{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{\text{min}})},K_{\epsilon}:=K_{M}+K_{R}+K_{L}.

Since
|\lambda-1|\geq\epsilon
which implies
|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq 2^{-1}\epsilon
for sufficiently large
u
and

O_{u}:=\{\lambda\geq\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}:|\lambda-\lambda_{u}^{*}|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\}.

Then

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\lambda-1|\geq\epsilon|X\geq x_{0})\leq\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda\in O_{u}|X\geq x_{0}).

We showed that

\int\limits_{\frac{x_{0}}{x_{u}}}^{\infty}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\geq\int\limits_{B_{u}}q_{u}(\lambda)d\lambda\geq C_{in}\eta_{u}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}e^{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})}.

So

\displaystyle\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda\in O_{u}|X\geq x_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K_{2}}{K_{1}}\frac{K_{\epsilon}\exp\{-x_{u}(\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})+C_{\text{min}})\}}{C_{in}\eta_{u}(1+A_{u})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-x_{u}\phi_{u}(\lambda_{u}^{*})\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{2}}{K_{1}}\frac{K_{\epsilon}}{C_{in}}\eta_{u}^{-1}(1+A_{u})^{\frac{p}{2}}e^{-x_{u}C_{\text{min}}}.

Recall
\eta_{u}^{-1}=\sqrt{x_{u}}
and
A_{u}=Cx_{u}^{m}
. Then
\eta_{u}^{-1}(1+A_{u})^{\frac{p}{2}}.
For sufficiently large
u
,
A_{u}\geq 1.
Hence
1+A_{u}\leq 2A_{u}=2Cx_{u}^{m}.
Then
(1+A_{u})^{\frac{p}{2}}\leq(2C)^{\frac{p}{2}}x_{u}^{\frac{mp}{2}}.
Then

\displaystyle\eta_{u}^{-1}(1+A_{u})^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq x_{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}(2C)^{\frac{p}{2}}x_{u}^{\frac{mp}{2}}

\displaystyle=(2C)^{\frac{p}{2}}x_{u}^{\frac{mp+1}{2}}

\displaystyle=C_{0}x_{u}^{B},C_{0}:=(2C)^{\frac{p}{2}},B:=\frac{mp+1}{2}.

Therefore

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda\in O_{u}|X\geq x_{0})\leq\frac{K_{2}}{K_{1}}\frac{K_{\epsilon}}{C_{in}}x_{u}^{B}e^{-C_{\text{min}}x_{u}}=C_{1}x_{u}^{B}e^{-C_{\text{min}}x_{u}}.

We know for sufficiently large
u,C_{1}x_{u}^{B}\leq e^{\frac{C_{\text{min}}}{2}x_{u}}.
Then

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(\lambda\in O_{u}|X\geq x_{0})\leq e^{\frac{C_{\text{min}}}{2}x_{u}}e^{-C_{\text{min}}x_{u}}=e^{-\frac{C_{\text{min}}}{2}x_{u}}.

Therefore,

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\lambda-1|\geq\epsilon|X\geq x_{0})\leq e^{-c^{{}^{\prime}}x_{u}},c^{\prime}:=\frac{C_{\text{min}}}{2}.

Then

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon|X\geq x_{0})\leq e^{-c^{{}^{\prime}}x_{u}}.

Note that

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon)=\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon,X<x_{0})+\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon,X\geq x_{0}).

Then

\displaystyle\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon,X\leq x_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(X<x_{0})

and

\displaystyle\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon,X\geq x_{0})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(X\geq x_{0})\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon|X\geq x_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon|X\geq x_{0}).

So

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon)\leq\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(X<x_{0})+e^{-c^{\prime}x_{u}}.

Note that
e^{-C_{0}u}\leq e^{-C_{0}Kx_{u}}
for sufficiently large
u
and constant
K
. Then for sufficiently large
u

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon)\leq e^{-c_{0}Kx_{u}}+e^{-c^{{}^{\prime}}x_{u}}=2e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}},c_{\epsilon}:=\min\{c_{0}K,c^{\prime}\}.

We want

2e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}\leq e^{-\frac{c_{\epsilon}}{2}x_{u}}\iff 2\leq e^{\frac{c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}{2}}\iff\log(2)\leq\frac{c_{\epsilon}}{2}x_{u}\iff\frac{2}{c_{\epsilon}}\log(2)\leq x_{u},

which is true for sufficiently large
u
. Therefore

\Pi_{u,\boldsymbol{k}}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon)\leq e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}.

That is

\mathbb{P}(|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\leq e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}.

Observe that

|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|<\epsilon\iff 1-\epsilon<\frac{X}{x_{u}}<1+\epsilon.

By taking complements

\{|\frac{X}{x_{u}}-1|\geq\epsilon\}=\{X\leq(1-\epsilon)x_{u}\}\cup\{X\geq(1+\epsilon)x_{u}\}.

Therefore

\mathbb{P}((1-\epsilon)x_{u}<X<(1+\epsilon)x_{u}|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\geq 1-e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}.

Note that

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)x_{u}<X<(1+\epsilon)x_{u}

\displaystyle\iff(1-\epsilon)^{m}x_{u}^{m}<X^{m}<(1+\epsilon)^{m}x_{u}^{m}

\displaystyle\iff C(1-\epsilon)^{m}x_{u}^{m}<CX^{m}<C(1+\epsilon)^{m}x_{u}^{m}.

Therefore,

\mathbb{P}(V\in[C(1-\epsilon)^{m}x_{u}^{m},C(1+\epsilon)^{m}x_{u}^{m}]|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\geq 1-e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}.

Observe that

Cx_{u}^{m}=C(\frac{u}{2C})^{\frac{m}{m+1}}=2^{-\frac{m}{m+1}}C^{\frac{1}{m+1}}u^{\frac{m}{m+1}}.

Recall
m=d-1
so
m(m+1)^{-1}=d^{-1}(d-1)=1-d^{-1}.
Therefore

Cx_{u}^{m}=2^{-(1-\frac{1}{d})}C^{\frac{1}{d}}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}=C_{d}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}},C_{d}:=2^{-(1-\frac{1}{d})}C^{\frac{1}{d}}

So

\mathbb{P}(V\asymp u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\geq 1-e^{-c_{\epsilon}x_{u}}.

Hence for sufficiently large
u

\mathbb{P}(V\asymp u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\geq\frac{1}{2}.

That is, there exists constants
c_{1,k},c_{2,k}>0
such that for sufficiently large
u

\mathbb{P}(c_{1,k}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}\leq V\leq c_{2,k}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{u}:=\{c_{1}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}\leq V\leq c_{2}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}\},c_{1}=\min\limits_{k}c_{1,k},c_{2}=\max\limits_{k}c_{2,k}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{u}|U=u)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{u}|U=u,\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k})\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k}|U=u)

\displaystyle\geq\sum\limits_{k}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k}|U=u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{k}\mathbb{P}(\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{k}|U=u)

Therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{u}|U=u)\geq\frac{1}{2}
.
Since
1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}\geq 0
and
(1+V)^{-1}\geq 0
,

\psi(u)=\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{1+V}|U=u]\geq\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{1+V}1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}|U=u].

On
\mathcal{A}_{u}
we have
V\leq c_{2}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}.
Since
V\rightarrow(1+V)^{-1}
is decreasing on
[0,\infty)
we have

\frac{1}{1+V}\geq\frac{1}{1+c_{2}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}}\text{ on }\mathcal{A}_{u}.

Equivalently

\frac{1}{1+V}1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}\geq\frac{1}{1+c_{2}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}}1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}.

Then,

\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{1+V}1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}|U=u]\geq\frac{1}{1+c_{2}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}}}\mathbb{E}[1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}|U=u].

Note that
\mathbb{E}[1_{\mathcal{A}_{u}}|U=u]=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{u}|U=u)\geq 2^{-1}
. Hence

\psi(u)\geq\frac{1}{2(1+c_{2}u^{1-\frac{1}{d}})},\text{ for sufficiently large }u.

Let
a:=1-d^{-1}\in(0,1).
For any
u
such that
c_{2}u^{a}\geq 1
iff
u\geq c_{2}^{-\frac{1}{a}}
which is true for sufficiently large
u
. Then
1+c_{2}u^{a}\leq 2c_{2}u^{a}
which implies
(1+c_{2}u^{a})^{-1}\geq(2c_{2}u^{a})^{-1}.
Hence for all sufficiently large
u,

\psi(u)\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{1+c_{2}u^{a}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2c_{2}u^{a}}=\frac{1}{4c_{2}}u^{-a}=\frac{1}{4c_{2}}u^{-(1-\frac{1}{d})}.

This implies

u\psi(u)\geq\frac{1}{4c_{2}}u^{\frac{1}{d}}\rightarrow\infty\text{ as }u\rightarrow\infty.

Then
\exists u_{*}<\infty
such that
\forall u\geq u_{*},u\psi(u)\geq(4c_{2})^{-1}u^{\frac{1}{d}}.
Now let
u_{0}:=\max\{u_{*},(16pc_{2})^{d}\}.
Then for any
u\geq u_{0}

u\psi(u)\geq\frac{1}{4c_{2}}u^{\frac{1}{d}}\geq\frac{1}{4c_{2}}u_{0}^{\frac{1}{d}}\geq\frac{1}{4c_{2}}(16pc_{2})=4p.

Recall
B(u)\geq\psi(u)(u\psi(u)-2p).
For
u\geq u_{0},u\psi(u)-2p\geq 2p
. Hence
u\geq u_{0}
implies

B(u)\geq 2p\psi(u)\geq\frac{2p}{4c_{2}}u^{\frac{1}{d}-1}=\frac{p}{2c_{2}}u^{\frac{1}{d}-1}.

Let
A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}:=\{u_{0}\leq U\leq b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}
with
b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
to be chosen. Note
B(u)\geq 0
is not guaranteed everywhere. Therefore we will lower bound the expectation on
A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}.
In addition, observe that
B(u)\geq-2p
. So,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c}}]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)\boldsymbol{1}_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]-2p\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c}).

On
A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}},U\geq u_{0}
, so
B(U)1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\geq(2c_{2})^{-1}pU^{\frac{1}{d}-1}1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}.
Now we will choose
b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and use the fact that
u\rightarrow u^{\frac{1}{d}-1}
is decreasing since
d\geq 2
. Thus on the event
\{U\leq b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}
,
U^{\frac{1}{d}-1}\geq b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\frac{1}{d}-1}
. Hence on
A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
,
U^{\frac{1}{d}-1}1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\geq b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\frac{1}{d}-1}1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}.
Therefore,
B(u)1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\geq p(2c_{2})^{-1}b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\frac{1}{d}-1}1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}.
Then,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]\geq\frac{p}{2c_{2}}b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\frac{1}{d}-1}\mathbb{E}[1_{A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}]-2p\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c})=\frac{p}{2c_{2}}b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{\frac{1}{d}-1}\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-2p\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c}).

We know
\boldsymbol{Y}=\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}
with
\boldsymbol{Z}\sim N_{p}(\boldsymbol{0}_{p},I_{p}).
The
U=||\boldsymbol{Y}||^{2}=||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}||^{2}.
By the triangle inequality
||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}||\leq||\boldsymbol{\theta}||+||\boldsymbol{Z}||.
By the reverse triangle inequality

|||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}||-||\boldsymbol{Z}|||\leq||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}-\boldsymbol{Z}||=||\boldsymbol{\theta}||.
This implies
||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}||\geq||\boldsymbol{\theta}||-||\boldsymbol{Z}||.
Therefore
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||-||\boldsymbol{Z}||\leq||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}||\leq||\boldsymbol{\theta}||+||\boldsymbol{Z}||.

This implies
(||\boldsymbol{\theta}||-||\boldsymbol{Z}||)^{2}\leq||\boldsymbol{\theta}+\boldsymbol{Z}||^{2}\leq(||\boldsymbol{\theta}||+||\boldsymbol{Z}||)^{2}.
Consider the event

E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}:=\{||\boldsymbol{Z}||\leq 2^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||\}.
On
E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}},U\geq(||\boldsymbol{\theta}-2^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||)^{2}=4^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}.
Similarly,

U\leq(||\boldsymbol{\theta}||+2^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||)^{2}=2.25||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}.
Now choose
b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=2.25||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}.
Then

E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\subseteq\{4^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\leq U\leq b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}.
Now pick any
\boldsymbol{\theta}
such that
4^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\geq u_{0}.
That is,

||\boldsymbol{\theta}||\geq 2\sqrt{u_{0}}.
This implies
E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\subseteq\{u_{0}\leq U\leq b_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}=A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}.
Therefore, whenever

||\boldsymbol{\theta}||\geq 2\sqrt{u_{0}},\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\geq\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})=\mathbb{P}(||\boldsymbol{Z}||\leq 2^{-1}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||).
Similarly,
\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(A_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c})
. Then,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]\geq\frac{p}{2C_{2}}(\frac{9}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2})^{\frac{1}{d}-1}\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-2p\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c}).

Let

C_{*}:=\frac{p}{2c_{2}}(\frac{9}{4})^{\frac{1}{d}-1}.

So,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]\geq C_{*}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-2p\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c}).

Let
S=||\boldsymbol{Z}||^{2}.
Then
S\sim\chi_{p}^{2}.
Observe

\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})=\mathbb{P}(S\leq(\frac{1}{2}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||)^{2})=\mathbb{P}(S\leq\frac{1}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2})=1-\mathbb{P}(S\geq\frac{1}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}).

Let
x=0.25||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}.
By Markov’s inequality,

\mathbb{P}(S\geq x)=\mathbb{P}(e^{\lambda S}\geq e^{\lambda x})\leq e^{-\lambda x}\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda S}]=e^{-\lambda x}(1-2\lambda)^{-\frac{p}{2}},\text{ for }\lambda<\frac{1}{2}.

Now optimize over
\lambda\in(0,2^{-1}).
Let

\phi(\lambda):=-\lambda x-\frac{p}{2}\log(1-2\lambda).

So
e^{-\lambda x}(1-2\lambda)^{-\frac{p}{2}}=e^{\phi(\lambda)}.
Then,

\phi^{\prime}(\lambda)=-x-\frac{p}{2}(-\frac{2}{1-2\lambda})=-x+\frac{p}{1-2\lambda}.

Then setting the first derivative equal to 0 gives

-x+\frac{p}{1-2\lambda}=0\implies 1-2\lambda=\frac{p}{x}\implies\lambda^{*}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{p}{x})\in(0,\frac{1}{2}).

Furthermore

\phi^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)=-p(1-2\lambda)^{-2}(-2)=\frac{2p}{(1-2\lambda)^{2}}>0.

Therefore
\lambda^{*}
minimizes
\phi
. Therefore,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda^{*}x}=\exp\{-\frac{x}{2}(1-\frac{p}{x})\}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{x-p}{2}\}

and

\displaystyle(1-2\lambda^{*})^{-\frac{p}{2}}=(\frac{p}{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle=(\frac{x}{p})^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Then

\mathbb{P}(S\geq x)\leq\exp\{-\frac{x-p}{2}\}(\frac{x}{p})^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S\geq\frac{1}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{-\frac{\frac{1}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}-p}{2}\}(\frac{\frac{1}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{p})^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}-4p}{8}\}(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p})^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{8}+\frac{p}{2}\}(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p})^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{8}+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{p}{2}\log(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p})\}.

Choose a constant e.g.
1/16
. Since
\log(x)/x\rightarrow 0
as
x\rightarrow\infty,\exists x_{0}>0
such that
\log(x)\leq x/8,\forall x\geq x_{0}
. Now choose
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||
large enough such that
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}/4p\geq x_{0}
. Then

\log(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p})\leq\frac{1}{8}\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p}=\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{32p}.

So

\frac{p}{2}\log(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p})\leq\frac{p}{2}\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{32p}=\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{64}.

Then

-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{8}+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{p}{2}\log(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{4p})\leq-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{8}+\frac{p}{2}+\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{64}=-\frac{7}{64}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}+\frac{p}{2}.

Then for large enough
\||\boldsymbol{\theta}||
such that
p/2\leq 3||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}/64
we obtain

-\frac{7}{64}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}+\frac{p}{2}\leq-\frac{7}{64}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}+\frac{3}{64}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\leq-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}.

That is, for sufficiently large
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||
,

\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c})\leq\exp\{-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\}.

So,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]

\displaystyle\geq C_{*}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}})-2p\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c})

\displaystyle=C_{*}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}(1-\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c}))-2p\mathbb{P}(E_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{c})

\displaystyle\geq C_{*}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}(1-\exp\{-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\})-2p\exp\{-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\}.

For sufficiently large
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||
,

\exp\{-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

So for sufficiently large
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||
,

\mathbb{E}[B(U)]\geq\frac{C_{*}}{2}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}-2p\exp\{-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\}.

We know

\exp\{\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{16}\}\geq\frac{1}{2}(\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{16})^{2}=\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{4}}{512}.

Hence

\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{16}\}\leq\frac{512}{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{4}}.

So

2p\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{16}\}\leq\frac{1024p}{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{4}}.

Now choose
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||
large enough such that
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2+\frac{2}{d}}\geq 4096p/C_{*}
. Then,

1024p\leq\frac{C_{*}}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2+\frac{2}{d}}.

This gives

\frac{1024p}{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{4}}\leq\frac{C_{*}}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}.

So for sufficiently large
||\boldsymbol{\theta}||

2p\exp\{-\frac{1}{16}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}\}\leq\frac{C_{*}}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{*}}{2}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}-\frac{C_{*}}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{*}}{4}||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{\frac{2}{d}-2}

\displaystyle>0.

Therefore
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(u)]>0
for sufficiently large
\boldsymbol{\theta}.
Thus by Stein’s identity

R(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\delta})=p+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[B(U)]>p\text{ for sufficiently large }||\boldsymbol{\theta}||.

Therefore
\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Theta}R(\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{\delta})>p.
Thus
\boldsymbol{\delta}
is not minimax.
∎

Appendix S3
Proofs of Section 3

S3.1
Proof of Theorem 3.1

Proof.

From the proof of Lemma 1.1

p_{d}^{lin}(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\boldsymbol{x})=[\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt_{\ell}\frac{t_{\ell}^{\frac{n_{\ell}}{2}-1}e^{-t_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\frac{n_{\ell}}{2})}](2^{d}\pi\kappa_{d}^{2})^{-\frac{p}{2}}(t_{1}\dots t_{d-1})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2^{d}\kappa_{d}^{2}t_{1}\dots t_{d-1}}\}.

Let
S:=t_{1}\dots t_{d-1}
and
W:=2^{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{2}S
. Then,
2^{d}\kappa_{d}^{2}S=2W
. Therefore,

\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2^{d}\kappa_{d}^{2}S}\}=\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2W}\}.

Furthermore,

(2\pi W)^{-\frac{p}{2}}=(2\pi 2^{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{2}S)^{-\frac{p}{2}}=(2^{d}\pi\kappa_{d}^{2})^{-\frac{p}{2}}S^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

Thus,

(2\pi W)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2W}\}=\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};0,WI_{p}),

where
\phi_{p}
is the Gaussian probability density function. Then,

p_{d}^{lin}(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\boldsymbol{x})=[\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}dt_{\ell}\frac{t_{\ell}^{\frac{n_{\ell}}{2}-1}e^{-t_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\frac{n_{\ell}}{2})}]\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};0,WI_{p}).

Observe that
f_{T_{\ell}}(t_{\ell}):=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}dt_{\ell}(\Gamma(\frac{n_{\ell}}{2}))^{-1}t_{\ell}^{\frac{n_{\ell}}{2}-1}e^{-t_{\ell}}
is the integral of the probability density function of
T_{\ell}\sim\Gamma(\frac{n_{\ell}}{2},1)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle p_{d}^{lin}(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\dots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(2^{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{2}\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}t_{\ell})I_{p})\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}f_{T_{\ell}}(t_{\ell})dt_{1}\dots dt_{d-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(2^{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{2}\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}T_{\ell})I_{p})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},WI_{p})].

That is,

\boldsymbol{\theta}|W\sim N_{p}(0,WI_{p}).

We know W is a function of S. Therefore,

\boldsymbol{\theta}|S\sim N_{p}(0,(2^{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{2}S)I_{p}).

Let
U:=2^{d-1}||\boldsymbol{x}||^{2}\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d}\sigma_{\ell}^{2}=2^{d-1}\kappa_{d}^{2}
. Then,

\boldsymbol{\theta}|U,S\sim N_{p}(\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(US)I_{p}).

Then for fixed k,

\boldsymbol{\theta}|U,S,k\sim N_{p}(\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(US)I_{p}),S=\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}T_{\ell},T_{\ell}\sim\Gamma(\frac{k_{\ell}}{2},1).

Let
b:=\frac{p}{2}-2>0\iff p\geq 5
which is the minimum dimension for the existence of proper minimax Bayes estimators. Now define
W\sim\mathrm{BetaPrime}(1,b)
where
h(w)=b(1+w)^{-(b+1)}=b(1+w)^{1-\frac{p}{2}}
. Now we define the hyperprior on
U
conditional on
(S,k)
by observing
U=WS^{-1}
. Note
f_{W|S=s,k}=h(w)
. Now,
w=us,u>0
. This implies
dw=sdu
. Therefore,

f_{U|S=s,k}(u|S,k)=f_{W|S=s,k}(w=us|S,k)|\frac{dw}{du}|=h(us)s,u>0.

Then,

f_{U|k}(u)=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}h(us)sf_{S|k}(s)ds\geq 0,u>0.

We also know

\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}f_{U|k}(u)du=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}h(us)sf_{S|k}(s)dsdu=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}h(w)dw)f_{S|k}(s)ds=1,

as
S
is a product of Gamma distributed random variables which have a proper probability density function. Therefore,
f_{U|k}
is a proper density. Observe that,
US=WS^{-1}S=W
. This implies
US\sim\mathrm{BetaPrime}(1,b)
. Hence,
\boldsymbol{\theta}|U,S,k\sim N_{p}(\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(US)I_{p})=N_{p}(\boldsymbol{0}_{p},WI_{p})
. Then,
\pi_{k}(\boldsymbol{\theta})=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})h(w)dw
, where the right hand side does not depend on k. So,

\displaystyle\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k}\omega_{k}\pi_{k}(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k}\omega_{k}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})h(w)dw

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})h(w)dw\sum\limits_{k}\omega_{k}.

For a given
\ell
,

\sum\limits_{k_{\ell}=1}^{n_{\ell}}\begin{pmatrix}n_{\ell}\\
k_{\ell}\end{pmatrix}=(\sum\limits_{k_{\ell=0}}^{n_{\ell}}\begin{pmatrix}n_{\ell}\\
k_{\ell}\end{pmatrix})-\begin{pmatrix}n_{\ell}\\
0\end{pmatrix}=2^{n_{\ell}}-1.

Hence,

\sum\limits_{k_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\dots\sum\limits_{k_{d-1}=1}^{n_{d-1}}\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}\begin{pmatrix}n_{\ell}\\
k_{\ell}\end{pmatrix}=\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}(\sum\limits_{k_{\ell}=1}^{n_{\ell}}\begin{pmatrix}n_{\ell}\\
k_{\ell}\end{pmatrix})=\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}(2^{n_{\ell}}-1).

Therefore,

N=\sum\limits_{k}\omega_{k}=\frac{\prod\limits_{\ell=1}^{d-1}(2^{n_{\ell}}-1)}{2^{n_{1}+\dots+n_{d-1}}}<\infty.

So,

\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})h(w)dw.

Then,

\displaystyle m(\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},I_{p})\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},I_{p})[\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})h(w)dw]d\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}[\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},I_{p})\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})d\theta]h(w)dw,\text{ by Tonelli's theorem,}

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)dw,\text{ by convolution of Gaussians}

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(2\pi(1+w))^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2(1+w)}\}h(w)dw

\displaystyle=Nb(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(1+w)^{1-p}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2(1+w)}\}dw

Let
u=(1+w)^{-1}
, so
w=(1-u)u^{-1},dw=-u^{-2}
, and
(1+w)^{1-p}=u^{p-1}
, Then,

\displaystyle m(\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=Nb(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{1}^{0}u^{p-1}\exp\{-\frac{u||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2}\}(-u^{-2})du

\displaystyle=Nb(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{1}u^{p-3}\exp\{-\frac{u||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2}\}du.

Let
C:=Nb(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}>0
and
I_{0}(\lambda):=\int\limits_{0}^{1}u^{p-3}e^{-\lambda u}du
with
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}r^{2}
and
r=||\boldsymbol{y}||
. Then
m(r)=CI_{0}(\lambda)
. Similarly, let
I_{1}(\lambda):=\int\limits_{0}^{1}u^{p-2}e^{-\lambda u}du
and
I_{2}(\lambda)=\int\limits_{0}^{1}u^{p-1}e^{-\lambda u}du
. Then,

\frac{d}{d\lambda}I_{0}(\lambda)=-I_{1}(\lambda)\text{ and }\frac{d}{d\lambda}I_{1}(\lambda)=-I_{2}(\lambda),

since
\frac{d}{d\lambda}(u^{a}e^{-\lambda u})=-u^{a+1}e^{-\lambda u}
and
|u^{a+1}e^{-\lambda u}|\leq u^{a+1}\in L^{1}(0,1),a>2
and using the Dominated Convergence Theorem. Then

m^{\prime}(r)=C\frac{dI_{0}}{d\lambda}\frac{d\lambda}{dr}=-CrI_{1}(\lambda)

and

\displaystyle m^{\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-CI_{1}(\lambda)-Cr\frac{d}{dr}\{I_{1}(\lambda)\}

\displaystyle=-CI_{1}(\lambda)+Cr^{2}I_{2}(\lambda).

For radial
m
in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
we know

\displaystyle\Delta m(r)

\displaystyle=m^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{p-1}{r}m^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-CI_{1}(\lambda)+Cr^{2}I_{2}(\lambda)+\frac{p-1}{r}(-CrI_{1}(\lambda))

\displaystyle=-CI_{1}(\lambda)+Cr^{2}I_{2}(\lambda)-C(p-1)I_{1}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=C[-I_{1}(\lambda)+r^{2}I_{2}(\lambda)-pI_{1}(\lambda)+I_{1}(\lambda)]

\displaystyle=C[r^{2}I_{2}(\lambda)-pI_{1}(\lambda)].

Observe

\nabla(\sqrt{m})=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}}\nabla m.

Then,

\Delta(\sqrt{m})=\nabla(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}}\nabla m)=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}}\Delta m+\nabla(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}})\cdot\nabla m.

We also have

\nabla(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}})=-\frac{1}{4}m^{-\frac{3}{2}}\nabla m.

Then

\Delta(\sqrt{m})=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{m}}\Delta m-\frac{1}{4}m^{-\frac{3}{2}}||\nabla m||^{2}\iff 4m^{\frac{3}{2}}\Delta(\sqrt{m})=2m\Delta m-||\nabla m||^{2}.

Thus,

\Delta(\sqrt{m})\leq 0\iff 2m\Delta m-||\nabla m||^{2}\leq 0\iff 2m\Delta m\leq||\nabla m||^{2}.

Let
m(\boldsymbol{y})=\tilde{m}(r)
. By the chain rule,

\frac{\partial m}{\partial y_{i}}=\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r)\frac{\partial r}{\partial y_{i}}=\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r)\frac{y_{i}}{r},r>0.

So,

\nabla m(\boldsymbol{y})=\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r)(\frac{y_{1}}{r},\dots,\frac{y_{p}}{r})=\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r)\frac{\boldsymbol{y}}{r}.

Then,

||\nabla m(\boldsymbol{y})||^{2}=(\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r))^{2}||\frac{\boldsymbol{y}}{r}||^{2}=(\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r))^{2}\frac{||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{r^{2}}=(\tilde{m}^{\prime}(r))^{2}=(m^{\prime}(r))^{2}.

Therefore,

\Delta\sqrt{m}\leq 0\iff 2m\Delta m\leq(m^{\prime})^{2}.

Thus,

\Delta\sqrt{m}\leq 0\iff 2C^{2}I_{0}(\lambda)(r^{2}I_{2}(\lambda)-pI_{1}(\lambda))\leq C^{2}r^{2}I_{1}(\lambda)^{2}.

That is,

2I_{0}(\lambda)(r^{2}I_{2}(\lambda)-pI_{1}(\lambda))\leq r^{2}I_{1}(\lambda)^{2}.

Recall
r^{2}=2\lambda
which gives

2I_{0}(\lambda)(2\lambda I_{2}(\lambda)-pI_{1}(\lambda))\leq 2\lambda I_{1}(\lambda)^{2}.

This becomes

I_{0}(\lambda)(2\lambda I_{2}(\lambda)-pI_{1}(\lambda))\leq\lambda I_{1}(\lambda)^{2}.

Let
a:=p-2
so
p=a+2
with
a\geq 3
. Also let
t=\lambda u
i.e.
u=t\lambda^{-1}
with
du=\lambda^{-1}dt
. Therefore,

\displaystyle I_{0}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{1}u^{p-2-1}e^{-\lambda u}du

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda}(\frac{t}{\lambda})^{a-1}e^{-t}dt

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-a}\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda}t^{a-1}e^{-t}dt

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-a}\gamma(a,\lambda),\text{ where }\gamma(.,.)\text{ is the lower incomplete gamma function. }

Similarly,
I_{1}(\lambda)=\lambda^{-(a+1)}\gamma(a+1,\lambda)
and
I_{2}(\lambda)=\lambda^{-(a+2)}\gamma(a+2,\lambda)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\lambda^{-a}\gamma(a,\lambda)(2\lambda\lambda^{-(a+2)}\gamma(a+2,\lambda)-p\lambda^{-(a+1)}\gamma(a+1,\lambda))\leq\lambda\lambda^{-2(a+1)}\gamma(a+1,\lambda)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\lambda^{-a}\gamma(a,\lambda)(2\lambda^{-(a+1)}\gamma(a+2,\lambda)-p\lambda^{-(a+1)}\gamma(a+1,\lambda))\leq\lambda^{-2a-1}\gamma(a+1,\lambda)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\lambda^{-a}\lambda^{-(a+1)}\gamma(a,\lambda)(2\gamma(a+2,\lambda))-p\gamma(a+1,\lambda))\leq\lambda^{-2a-1}\gamma(a+1,\lambda)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\lambda^{-2a-1}\gamma(a,\lambda)(2\gamma(a+2,\lambda)-p\gamma(a+1,\lambda))\leq\lambda^{-2a-1}\gamma(a+1,\lambda)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\gamma(a,\lambda)(2\gamma(a+2,\lambda)-(a+2)\gamma(a+1,\lambda))\leq\gamma(a+1,\lambda)^{2}.

Note that

\gamma(a+1,\lambda)=a\gamma(a,\lambda)-\lambda^{a}e^{-\lambda}\text{ and }\gamma(a+2,\lambda)=(a+1)\gamma(a+1,\lambda)-\lambda^{a+1}e^{-\lambda}.

Let
G:=\gamma(a,\lambda)
and
E:=\lambda^{a}e^{-\lambda}
. Then,
\gamma(a+1,\lambda)=aG-E
. Furthermore,
\gamma(a+2,\lambda)=(a+1)(aG-E)-\lambda E=a(a+1)G-(a+1+\lambda)E
. Therefore,

\displaystyle G(2[a(a+1)G-(a+1+\lambda)E]-(a+2)[aG-E])\leq(aG-E)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle G(2a(a+1)G-2(a+1+\lambda)E-(a+2)aG+(a+2)E)\leq(aG-E)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle G([2a(a+1)-(a+2)a]G+[a+2-2(a+1+\lambda)]E)\leq(aG-E)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle G([2(a+1)-(a+2)]aG+[a+2-2a-2-2\lambda]E)\leq(aG-E)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle G([2a+2-a-2]aG+[-a-2\lambda]E)\leq(aG-E)^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle G(a^{2}G-(a+2\lambda)E)\leq a^{2}G^{2}-2aGE+E^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle a^{2}G^{2}-aGE-2\lambda GE\leq a^{2}G^{2}-2aGE+E^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle 0\leq-aGE+2\lambda GE+E^{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle 0\leq E^{2}+(2\lambda-a)GE

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle 0\leq E(E+(2\lambda-a)G)

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle 0\leq E+(2\lambda-a)G

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle 0\leq\lambda^{a}e^{-\lambda}+(2\lambda-a)\gamma(a,\lambda)=:F

Note that
G^{\prime}(\lambda)=\lambda^{a-1}e^{-\lambda}
and
E^{\prime}(\lambda)=a\lambda^{a-1}e^{-\lambda}-\lambda^{a}e^{-\lambda}=\lambda^{a-1}e^{-\lambda}(a-\lambda)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle F^{\prime}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{a-1}e^{-\lambda}(a-\lambda)+2\gamma(a,\lambda)+(2\lambda-a)\lambda^{a-1}e^{-\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{a-1}e^{-\lambda}[a-\lambda+2\lambda-a]+2\gamma(a,\lambda)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{a}e^{-\lambda}+2\gamma(a,\lambda)

\displaystyle>0,\forall\lambda>0.

Recall,
\gamma(a,\lambda)=\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda}t^{a-1}e^{-t}dt
. For
0\leq t\leq\lambda,e^{-t}
is decreasing. Therefore,
e^{-\lambda}\leq e^{-t}\leq 1
. Then,
e^{-\lambda}\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda}t^{a-1}dt\leq\gamma(a,\lambda)\leq\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda}t^{a-1}dt
. Observe that
\int\limits_{0}^{\lambda}t^{a-1}dt=a^{-1}\lambda^{a}
. Therefore,

e^{-\lambda}\frac{\lambda^{a}}{a}\leq\gamma(a,\lambda)\leq\frac{\lambda^{a}}{a}\iff e^{-\lambda}\leq\frac{\gamma(a,\lambda)}{\frac{\lambda^{a}}{a}}\leq 1.

This implies,

\lim\limits_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\frac{\gamma(a,\lambda)}{\frac{\lambda^{a}}{a}}=1\text{ i.e }\lim\limits_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\frac{\gamma(a,\lambda)}{\lambda^{a}}=\frac{1}{a}.

Note that

\frac{F(\lambda)}{\lambda^{a}}=e^{-\lambda}+(2\lambda-a)\frac{\gamma(a,\lambda)}{\lambda^{a}}.

Then,

\lim\limits_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\frac{F(\lambda)}{\lambda^{a}}=1-\frac{a}{a}=0.

Since
F^{\prime}(\lambda)>0,\forall\lambda>0
and
\lim\limits_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}F(\lambda)=0
we have that
F(\lambda)>0,\forall\lambda>0
. Therefore,
\Delta\sqrt{m(\boldsymbol{y})}\leq 0,\forall\boldsymbol{y}\neq\boldsymbol{0}_{p}
. Recall,

m(\boldsymbol{y})=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)dw.

For
j=1,\dots,p
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\{(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2(1+w)}\}\}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2(1+w)}\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{y_{j}}{(1+w)}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p}).

Consider compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
M:=\sup\limits_{x\in K}|x_{j}|<\infty
. Since

\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}}{2(1+w)}\}\leq 1\text{ we know }\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})\leq(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

Hence,
\forall\boldsymbol{y}\in K

\displaystyle|\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)|

\displaystyle=\frac{|y_{j}|}{1+w}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};0,(1+w)I_{p})h(w)

\displaystyle\leq M(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}h(w),M=|y_{j}|.

Let
g(w):=M(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}h(w)
. Then
g(w)\geq 0
and integrable since
(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}\leq 1
and
h
is a probability density function. That is,

\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}g(w)dw\leq M(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}h(w)dw=M(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}<\infty.

Let
\boldsymbol{e}_{j}
be the j-th unit vector and fix
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. For
t\neq 0
,

\frac{m(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j})-m(\boldsymbol{y})}{t}=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w)-f(\boldsymbol{y},w)}{t}dw

where

f(\boldsymbol{y},w):=\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w).

Let
\psi(s)=f(\boldsymbol{y}+s\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w).
By the mean value theorem

\frac{f(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w)-f(\boldsymbol{y},w)}{t}=\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}f(\boldsymbol{y}+\eta t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w),\text{ for some }\eta\in(0,1).

Now, choose
K
containing
\{\boldsymbol{y}+s\boldsymbol{e}_{j}:|s|\leq 1\}
. Then,
\forall|t|\leq 1
we have
\boldsymbol{y}+\eta t\boldsymbol{e}_{j}\in K
. Then,

|\frac{f(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w)-f(\boldsymbol{y},w)}{t}|=|\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}f(\boldsymbol{y}+\eta t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w)|\leq g(w).

For fixed
w
, as
t\rightarrow 0

\frac{f(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w)-f(\boldsymbol{y},w)}{t}\rightarrow\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}f(\boldsymbol{y},w).

Then by the dominated convergence theorem,

\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0}\frac{m(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j})-m(\boldsymbol{y})}{t}=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\lim\limits_{t\rightarrow 0}\frac{f(\boldsymbol{y}+t\boldsymbol{e}_{j},w)-f(\boldsymbol{y},w)}{t}dw=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}f(\boldsymbol{y},w)dw.

That is,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}m(\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}f(\boldsymbol{y},w)dw

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)dw

\displaystyle=-Ny_{j}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1+w}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)dw.

Note that for
i\neq j
,

\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})=\frac{y_{i}y_{j}}{(1+w)^{2}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p}),

and if
i=j
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y_{i}^{2}}\phi_{p}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\{\frac{y_{i}}{(1+w)}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};0,(1+w)I_{p})\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(1+w)}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\{y_{i}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{(1+w)}[\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})-\frac{y_{i}^{2}}{(1+w)}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})]

\displaystyle=(\frac{y_{i}^{2}}{(1+w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{1+w})\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p}).

For
i\neq j
,

\displaystyle|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)|

\displaystyle=\frac{|y_{i}y_{j}|}{(1+w)^{2}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M^{2}}{(1+w)^{2}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M^{2}}{(1+w)^{2}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}}h(w)

\displaystyle=M^{2}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-2}h(w)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}h(w),

and for
i=j
,

\displaystyle|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y_{i}^{2}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})|

\displaystyle=|\frac{y_{i}^{2}}{(1+w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{1+w}|\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)

\displaystyle\leq(\frac{y_{i}^{2}}{(1+w)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1+w})\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)

\displaystyle\leq(\frac{M^{2}}{(1+w)^{2}}+\frac{1}{1+w})(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}}h(w)

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}[M^{2}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-2}+(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}]h(w)

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(M^{2}+1)(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}h(w).

Then,

|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)|\leq C_{k}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}(1+w)^{-\frac{p}{2}-1}h(w),C_{k}=(1+M^{2}).

Therefore, by a similar dominated convergence theorem argument,
m\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
. Since
m\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p}),m(\boldsymbol{y})>0
, and
\phi(t)=\sqrt{t}
is
C^{\infty}((0,\infty))
, so
\sqrt{m}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
. Then
\Delta\sqrt{m}
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. We know
\Delta\sqrt{m(\boldsymbol{y})}\leq 0,\forall\boldsymbol{y}\neq\boldsymbol{0}_{p}
. Now let
\boldsymbol{y}_{n}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{0}_{p}
with
\boldsymbol{y}_{n}\neq\boldsymbol{0}_{p}
. By continuity,
\Delta\sqrt{m}(0)=\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Delta\sqrt{m}(\boldsymbol{y}_{n})\leq 0
. That is
\Delta\sqrt{m}(\boldsymbol{y})\leq 0,\forall\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
Now,

\nabla m(\boldsymbol{y})=-N\boldsymbol{y}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1+w}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};0,(1+w)I_{p})h(w)dw.

Now,

\pi(w|y):=\frac{\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)}{\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{y};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},(1+w)I_{p})h(w)dw}.

Then,

\frac{\nabla m(\boldsymbol{y})}{m(\boldsymbol{y})}=-\boldsymbol{y}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1+w}\pi(w|\boldsymbol{y})dw=-\boldsymbol{y}\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{1+W}|\boldsymbol{Y}=\boldsymbol{y}].

Since

0<\frac{1}{1+w}\leq 1\text{ we have }0<\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{1+W}|\boldsymbol{Y}=\boldsymbol{y}]\leq 1.

Therefore,

||\frac{\nabla m(\boldsymbol{y})}{m(\boldsymbol{y})}||=||\boldsymbol{y}||\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{1+W}|\boldsymbol{Y}=\boldsymbol{y}]\leq||\boldsymbol{y}||.

This implies,

||\frac{\nabla m(\boldsymbol{y})}{m(\boldsymbol{y})}||^{2}\leq||\boldsymbol{y}||^{2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[||\frac{\nabla m(\boldsymbol{Y})}{m(\boldsymbol{Y})}||^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[||\boldsymbol{Y}||^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}[\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}Y_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[Y_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{p}(1+\theta_{j}^{2})

\displaystyle=(p+||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2})

\displaystyle<\infty.

Then by Theorem 3.1 in
[
11
]

\delta_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{Y})=\boldsymbol{Y}+\frac{\nabla m(\boldsymbol{Y})}{m(\boldsymbol{Y})}\text{ is minimax. }

∎

S3.2
Proof of Corollary 3.2

Proof.

From the proof of Theorem 3.1 the prior density can be written as

\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},wI_{p})h(w)dw,\text{ where }h(w)=b(1+w)^{-(b+1)},b=\frac{p}{2}-2.

That is

\displaystyle\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=N\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\det(wI_{p})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{T}(wI_{p})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\theta}\}h(w)dw

\displaystyle=N(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}w^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2w}\}h(w)dw.

This is exactly of the form of expression (3.4) in
[
11
]
. Note that

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\infty}\frac{h(w)}{bw^{-(b+1)}}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\infty}\frac{b(1+w)^{-(b+1)}}{bw^{-(b+1)}}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(1+w)^{-(b+1)}}{w^{-(b+1)}}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\infty}(\frac{1+w}{w})^{-(b+1)}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{w\rightarrow\infty}(\frac{1}{w}+1)^{-(b+1)}

\displaystyle=1.

That is
h(w)\sim bw^{-(b+1)}
. In view of Theorem 3.15 from
[
11
]

a=-(b+1)=-(\frac{p}{2}-2+1)=1-\frac{p}{2}<-1\iff 4<p
which is true for our case of
p\geq 5
. Therefore, by Theorem 3.15 of
[
11
]
the induced decision rule is admissible.
∎

Appendix S4
Proofs of Section 4

S4.1
Proof of Theorem 4.1

Proof.

Fix
v>0
. Consider

m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)=\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},vI_{p})\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta}),

where

\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},vI_{p})=(2\pi v)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2v}\}.

Now define the push forward measure
\pi^{(v)}(A):=\pi(\sqrt{v}A),A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Let
\boldsymbol{w}:=v^{-\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{z}
. For
\boldsymbol{\theta}=\sqrt{v}\boldsymbol{\eta}
. Then

\displaystyle m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(2\pi v)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2v}\}\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=v^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{z}-\sqrt{v}\boldsymbol{\eta}||^{2}}{2v}\}\pi^{(v)}(d\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=v^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\sqrt{v}\boldsymbol{w}-\sqrt{v}\boldsymbol{\eta}||^{2}}{2v}\}\pi^{(v)}(d\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=v^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\sqrt{v}(\boldsymbol{w}-\boldsymbol{\eta})||^{2}}{2v}\}\pi^{(v)}(d\boldsymbol{\eta})

\displaystyle=v^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}(2\pi)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{w}-\boldsymbol{\eta}||^{2}}{2}\}\pi^{(v)}(d\boldsymbol{\eta}).

That is

m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)=v^{-\frac{p}{2}}m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\frac{\boldsymbol{z}}{\sqrt{v}};1).

Then

\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}=\sqrt{v^{-\frac{p}{2}}m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\frac{\boldsymbol{z}}{\sqrt{v}};1)}=v^{-\frac{p}{4}}\sqrt{m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\boldsymbol{w};1)}.

Let
g(\boldsymbol{w}):=\sqrt{m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\boldsymbol{w};1)}
. Then
\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}=v^{-\frac{p}{4}}g(\boldsymbol{w})
. Since

\boldsymbol{w}=\frac{\boldsymbol{z}}{\sqrt{v}},\frac{\partial w_{i}}{\partial z_{j}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{v}}\delta_{ij}.

Furthermore

\nabla_{\boldsymbol{z}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}=v^{-\frac{p}{4}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{v}}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{w}}g(\boldsymbol{w})=v^{-\frac{p}{4}-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{w}}g(\boldsymbol{w}).

Observe

\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z_{i}^{2}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}=v^{-\frac{p}{4}-\frac{1}{2}}v^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial w_{i}^{2}}g(\boldsymbol{w})=v^{-\frac{p}{4}-1}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial w_{i}^{2}}g(\boldsymbol{w}).

Then

\displaystyle\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z_{i}^{2}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}v^{-\frac{p}{4}-1}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial w_{i}^{2}}g(\boldsymbol{w})

\displaystyle=v^{-\frac{p}{4}-1}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial w_{i}^{2}}g(\boldsymbol{w})

\displaystyle=v^{-\frac{p}{4}-1}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{w}}g(\boldsymbol{w}).

That is

\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}=v^{-\frac{p}{4}-1}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{w}}\sqrt{m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\boldsymbol{w};1)},\boldsymbol{w}=\frac{\boldsymbol{z}}{\sqrt{v}}.

Note that

\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}\leq 0\iff v^{-\frac{p}{4}-1}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{w}}\sqrt{m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\boldsymbol{w};1)}\leq 0\iff\Delta_{\boldsymbol{w}}\sqrt{m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\boldsymbol{w};1)}\leq 0.

By Theorem 3.1
\Delta_{\boldsymbol{w}}\sqrt{m_{\pi^{(v)}}(\boldsymbol{w};1)}\leq 0,\forall\boldsymbol{w}
. Therefore
\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}\leq 0,\forall\boldsymbol{z},\forall v>0
. In particular,

\Delta_{\boldsymbol{z}}\sqrt{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)}\leq 0,\forall v\in[v_{w},v_{x}].

Note that for fixed
\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p},v>0,

0<\exp\{-\frac{||\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}}{2v}\}\leq 1.

Therefore,

0<\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},vI_{p})\leq(2\pi v)^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

Then

0\leq m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)=\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\phi_{p}(\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{\theta};\boldsymbol{0}_{p},vI_{p})\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq(2\pi v)^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})=N(2\pi v)^{-\frac{p}{2}}

since
\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})
is a finite measure. Therefore

0\leq m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)\leq N(2\pi v)^{-\frac{p}{2}}<\infty.

Thus
m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{z};v)
is finite for every
\boldsymbol{z}
and every
v>0
. Then by Theorem 1 (ii) of
[
14
]

\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})
is minimax.
∎

S4.2
Proof of Corollary 4.2

Proof.

Consider
A_{0}=\{g:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},g\geq 0\text{ and }\int g(\boldsymbol{y})d\boldsymbol{y}=1\}
. We know
\mathbb{\pi}(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0,\forall\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. The predictive Bayes rule in view of
[
4
]
can be written as

\hat{p}_{M}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})M(d\boldsymbol{\theta})}{\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})M(d\boldsymbol{\theta})}=\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})M(d\boldsymbol{\theta}|\boldsymbol{x}).

We know
p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq(2\pi v_{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}
and
p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}
. Then,

p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\leq(2\pi v_{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

This implies

\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{\theta}\leq(2\pi v_{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{\theta}=N(2\pi v_{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}<\infty.

Similarly,

\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{\theta}\leq(2\pi v_{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{\theta}=N(2\pi v_{x})^{-\frac{p}{2}}<\infty.

From
[
1
]

\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})
minimizes the average KL risk with respect to the finite prior measure
\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})

B_{KL}(\pi,\hat{p})=\int R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\hat{p})\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta}).

Assume for contradiction there exists some predictive rule
\tilde{p}
which dominates
\hat{p}_{\pi}
. Then

R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{p})\leq R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\hat{p}_{\pi}),\forall\boldsymbol{\theta},

with strict inequality for at least one
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. Integrating against
\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{\theta}
gives

B_{KL}(\pi,\tilde{p})\leq B_{KL}(\pi,\hat{p}_{\pi}).

But we also know that
\hat{p}_{\pi}
minimizes
B_{KL}(\pi,\cdot)
. Hence
B_{KL}(\pi,\tilde{p})=B_{KL}(\pi,\hat{p}_{\pi})
. Then

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=B_{KL}(\pi,\tilde{p})-B_{KL}(\pi,\hat{p}_{\pi})

\displaystyle=\int\int\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\log(\frac{p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{x}\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle-\int\int\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\log(\frac{p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})}{\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{x}\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})

\displaystyle=\int\int\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\log(\frac{\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{x}\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta}).

Now we will show that

\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})|\log(\frac{\hat{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})|d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}<\infty.

By the triangle inequality it suffices to show

\displaystyle\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int

\displaystyle p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})|\log(\hat{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}))|d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int

\displaystyle p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})|\log(\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}))|d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}<\infty.

Let
(\log(t))^{+}:=\max\{\log(t),0\}
and
(\log(t))^{-}:=\max\{-\log(t),0\}
with

\log(t)=(\log(t))^{+}-(\log(t))^{-}
. Then for any density function

q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}),(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{+}\leq q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}).
Therefore,

\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{+}d\boldsymbol{y}\leq(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}\int q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})d\boldsymbol{y}=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}.

Then,

\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{+}d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}\leq N(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}<\infty,

which holds for both
q=\hat{p}_{\pi}
and
q=\tilde{p}
. Observe
(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{-}=(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{+}-\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}))
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{-}d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{+}d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}-\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})[\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\log(\frac{p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})}{q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}-\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\log(p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta}))d\boldsymbol{y}]d\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)-\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\log(p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta}))d\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)+\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi v_{y})+\frac{1}{2v_{y}}\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})||\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{-\theta}||^{2}d\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)+\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi v_{y})+\frac{1}{2v_{y}}\mathbb{E}[||\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{\theta}||^{2}]

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)+\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi v_{y})+\frac{1}{2v_{y}}tr(v_{y}I_{p})

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)+\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi v_{y})+\frac{p}{2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)+\frac{p}{2}[log(2\pi v_{y})+\log(e)]

\displaystyle=(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)+\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi ev_{y}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})(\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})))^{-}d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq N(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+\int R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},q)\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})+N\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi ev_{y})

\displaystyle=N(2\pi v_{y})^{-\frac{p}{2}}+B_{KL}(\pi,q)+N\frac{p}{2}\log(2\pi ev_{y})

\displaystyle<\infty,

since
B_{KL}(\pi,\hat{p}_{\pi})<\infty
by Theorem 4.1 and
B_{KL}(\pi,\tilde{p})\leq B_{KL}(\pi,\hat{p})
by assumption. Since both positive and negative parts are finite and
|\log(t)|=(\log(t))^{+}+(\log(t))^{-}
,

\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})|\log(q(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}))|d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}<\infty.

Thus,

\int\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})|\log(\frac{\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})|d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}<\infty.

Then by Fubini’s theorem

0=\int[\int\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})]\log(\frac{\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta}|\boldsymbol{x})d\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\frac{p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})}{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})}d\boldsymbol{\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})}\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta}).

Then,

\int p(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{\theta})p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})\pi(d\boldsymbol{\theta})=m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}).

So,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int\int m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})\log(\frac{\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}d\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})[\int\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})\log(\frac{\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})}{\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})})d\boldsymbol{y}]d\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})KL(\hat{p}_{\pi},\tilde{p})d\boldsymbol{x}.

Since the integrand is non-negative, it follows that

KL(\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}),\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}))=0\text{ for }m_{\pi}-a.e.\boldsymbol{x}.

Hence, for
m_{\pi}-a.e.\boldsymbol{x}
,

\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})=\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x}),a.e.\boldsymbol{y}.

Note that
m_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{x})>0,\forall\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
because
p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})>0
and
\pi(\boldsymbol{\theta})>0
. Therefore,
m_{\pi}-a.e.\boldsymbol{x}
implies Lebesgue-a.e.
\boldsymbol{x}
. Since
p(\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})d\boldsymbol{x}=p(d\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure, for every
\boldsymbol{\theta}
,

\tilde{p}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})=\hat{p}_{\pi}(\boldsymbol{y}|\boldsymbol{x})a.e.\text{ in }\boldsymbol{y},p(d\boldsymbol{x}|\boldsymbol{\theta})-a.e.\boldsymbol{x}.

Therefore, for every
\boldsymbol{\theta},

R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\tilde{p})=R_{KL}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\hat{p}_{\pi}).

This contradicts strict domination which requires strict inequality for at least one
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. Therefore, no such
\tilde{p}
exists and
\hat{p}_{\pi}
is admissible.
∎
